as multiplication by
\frac{\alpha\beta}{p^{r_{2}+1}}
.

(2)

The image of
\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\coh,-D}
under the extension-by-zero map is
\eta^{\mathrm{alg}}_{-D}
.

This class enjoys the following additional properties:

(3)

The operator
U^{\prime}_{\Kl,1}
acts on
\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\coh,-D}
as multiplication by
\alpha+\beta
.

(4)

The spherical Hecke algebra acts via the system of eigenvalues associated to
\Pi^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Result from
[
Pil20
]
.
∎

Definition 0.11.6.4
.

Let
\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\coh}
be the image of
\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\coh,-D}
under the natural map

H^{2}_{c}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},L_{2}(-D)\right)\rTo H^{2}_{c}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},L_{2}\right).

This enjoys analogues of properties (1)–(4) (
mutatis mutandis
).

Definition 0.11.6.5
.

Let
\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh,-D}
be the image of
\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\coh,-D}
under the restriction map to
H^{2}_{c0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{1}(-D)\right)
. For future use, we define
\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh}
to be the image of
\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh,-D}
in
H^{2}_{c0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{1}\right)
.

Proposition 0.11.6.6
.

The class
P(\Phi)\cdot\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh,-D}
lies in the kernel of
Z^{\prime}
, where
P(T)
denotes the quadratic polynomial
(1-\tfrac{T}{\alpha})(1-\tfrac{T}{\beta})
.

Proof.

We know that
\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh,-D}
is an eigenvector for
U^{\prime}_{2}
with eigenvalue
\alpha\beta/p^{r_{2}+1}
, and for
Z^{\prime}+\Phi
with eigenvalue
\alpha+\beta
. Using the identity (
0.11.1
) the result follows formally.
∎

Remark 0.11.6.7
.

This result is somewhat analogous to a step in the proof of the regulator formula for Rankin–Selberg convolutions, where one shows that the image of an eigenform under a suitable quadratic polynomial in Frobenius is
p
-depleted
, i.e. in the kernel of the operator
U_{p}
. As we shall see later, the kernel of
Z^{\prime}
is the appropriate analogue in our context of
p
-depletedness.

0.12.
fp-cohomology and coherent fp-pairs for
G

0.12.1.
The dual BGG complex

Definition 0.12.1.1
.

Define the
dual BGG complex associated to
\mathcal{V}
to be

\BGG(\mathcal{V}):\mathcal{N}^{0}\rTo^{\nabla^{0}}\mathcal{N}^{1}\rTo^{\nabla^{1}}\mathcal{N}^{2}\rTo^{\nabla^{2}}\mathcal{N}^{3},

where the differentials are given by certain homogeneous differential operators of degrees
r_{2}+1
,
r_{1}-r_{2}+1
and
r_{2}+1
, respectively (c.f.
[
Til12
, §7]
.

We equip it with the following filtration:

{\mathscr{F}il}^{n}\BGG(\mathcal{V})=\begin{cases}\mathcal{N}^{0}\rTo\mathcal{N}^{1}\rTo\mathcal{N}^{2}\rTo\mathcal{N}^{3}\qquad&\text{if $n\leqslant 0$}\\
0\rTo\mathcal{N}^{1}\rTo\mathcal{N}^{2}\rTo\mathcal{N}^{3}&\text{if $1\leqslant n\leqslant r_{2}+1$}\\
0\rTo 0\rTo\mathcal{N}^{2}\rTo\mathcal{N}^{3}&\text{if $r_{2}+2\leqslant n\leqslant r_{1}+2$}\\
0\rTo 0\rTo 0\rTo\mathcal{N}^{3}&\text{if $r_{1}+3\leqslant n\leqslant r_{1}+r_{2}+3$}\\
0&\text{if $r_{1}+r_{2}+4\leqslant n$}.\end{cases}

We define
\BGG_{c}(\mathcal{V})
to be the subcomplex with
\mathcal{N}^{i}
replaced by
\mathcal{N}^{i}(-D)
.

Proposition 0.12.1.2
.

The dual BGG complex
\BGG(\mathcal{V})
is a direct summand of the logarithmic de Rham complex
\DR(\mathcal{V})\coloneqq\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega^{\bullet}\langle D\rangle
(in the category of abelian sheaves over
X_{G,\Kl,\mathbf{Q}_{p}}
). The inclusion is Hecke equivariant, and the projection map is a quasi-isomorphism of filtered complexes. The same holds for
\BGG_{c}(\mathcal{V})
and
\DR_{c}(\mathcal{V})
.

Proof.

See
[
Til12
, §7]
for the statement for
\BGG(\mathcal{V})
. For the version with compact support, see
[
LP18
, §5.4]
.
∎

Definition 0.12.1.3
.

For
i,j\geqslant 0
,
n\in\mathbf{Z}
we define

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\BGG_{c}(\mathcal{V}),n)

\displaystyle=H^{j}_{c}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},{\mathscr{F}il}^{n}\mathcal{N}^{i}(-\mathcal{D})\right),

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\BGG_{c}(\mathcal{V}))

\displaystyle=H_{c}^{j}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{i}(-\mathcal{D})\right),

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),n)

\displaystyle=H^{j}_{c}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},{\mathscr{F}il}^{n}\mathcal{N}^{i}\right),

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{c}^{i,j}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\BGG(\mathcal{V}))

\displaystyle=H^{j}_{c}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{i}\right),

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),n)

\displaystyle=H^{j}_{c0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},{\mathscr{F}il}^{n}\mathcal{N}^{i}\right),

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}))

\displaystyle=H^{j}_{c0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{N}^{i}\right)

Note 0.12.1.4
.

If
n\geqslant 1
, which is the case which will interest us, we have

\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\BGG_{c}(\mathcal{V}),n)=\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG_{c}(\mathcal{V}),n)=0

unless
i\in\{1,2,3\}
and
j\in\{2,3\}
, since
{\mathscr{F}il}^{n}\mathcal{N}^{i}
is zero unless
1\leqslant i\leqslant 3
, and the functors
H^{j}_{c}(\mathcal{X}^{\geqslant 1}_{G,\Kl},-)
and
H^{j}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{G,\Kl},-)
vanish on canonical vector bundles unless
j\in\{2,3\}
by
Corollary

0.11.5.4
and
Corollary

0.11.5.5
.

For
\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\BGG_{c}(\mathcal{V})(-\mathcal{D}),n)
we have a slightly weaker result: the non-zero terms are in degrees
1\leqslant i\leqslant 3
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant 3
, since it is obvious that
H^{0}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},-)
vanishes for any locally free sheaf.

Note 0.12.1.5
.

Definition
0.12.1.3
also makes sense when we replace
\BGG_{\star}(\mathcal{V})
by
\DR_{\star}(V)
and
\mathcal{N}^{i}
by
\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega_{G}^{i}
. By Proposition
0.12.1.2
, we obtain natural maps from the ‘BGG-version’ of the groups to the respective ‘de Rham’ versions.

Proposition 0.12.1.6
.

We have first-quadrant cohomological spectral sequences, starting at the
E_{1}
page (with differentials on the
E_{1}
page given by
\nabla
):

(0.12.1)

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\BGG_{c}(\mathcal{V}),n)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{i+j}_{\mathrm{dR},c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle,\mathcal{V},n),

(0.12.2)

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),n)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{i+j}_{\mathrm{dR},c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{V},n),

(0.12.3)

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),n)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{i+j}_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V},n)

which are compatible under the restriction map
\mathrm{res}^{\mathrm{ord}}
. If
n\geqslant 1
, all three spectral sequences degenerate at
E_{3}
. Similarly, for the unfiltered complexes we have Frölicher spectral sequences

(0.12.4)

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\BGG_{c}(\mathcal{V}))

\displaystyle\Rightarrow H^{i+j}_{\mathrm{rig},c}(X_{G,\Kl,0}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-D_{0}\rangle,\mathcal{V}),

(0.12.5)

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\BGG(\mathcal{V}))

\displaystyle\Rightarrow H^{i+j}_{\mathrm{rig},c}(X_{G,\Kl,0}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{V}),

(0.12.6)

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}))

\displaystyle\Rightarrow H^{i+j}_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V}).

Proof.

In each case, the spectral sequence arises as one of the spectral sequences associated to a suitable double complex computing
\widetilde{H}^{\bullet}
. The degeneration follows from the fact that the
E_{1}
terms are zero outside
1\leqslant i\leqslant 3
.
∎

Notation 0.12.1.7
.

We denote the
E_{2}
pages of these spectral sequences by
\mathcal{H}^{ij}_{?}(\dots)
, so
\mathcal{H}^{ij}_{?}(\dots)
is the
i
-th cohomology of the complex
\mathscr{C}^{\bullet j}_{?}(\dots)
.

Corollary 0.12.1.8
.

Let
-\leqslant q\leqslant r_{2}
. Then the edge maps at
(1,2)
of the spectral sequences (
0.12.2
) amd (
0.12.3
) are isomorphisms

(0.12.7a)

\displaystyle\alpha_{G,\mathrm{rig},c}:\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{V},1+q)

\displaystyle\rTo_{\cong}\mathcal{H}^{1,2}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),1+q)=H_{c}^{2}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{1}\right)^{\nabla=0},

(0.12.7b)

\displaystyle\alpha_{G,\mathrm{rig},c0}:\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V},1+q)

\displaystyle\rTo_{\cong}\mathcal{H}^{1,2}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),1+q)=H_{c0}^{2}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{N}^{1}\right)^{\nabla=0}.

The spectral sequence
\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\BGG_{c}(\mathcal{V})(-D),1+q)
gives an exact sequence

(0.12.7c)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dfrac{H_{c}^{1}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{2}(-\mathcal{D})\right)^{\nabla=0}}{\nabla H_{c}^{1}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{1}(-\mathcal{D})\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle,\mathcal{V},1+q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{c}^{2}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{1}(-\mathcal{D})\right)^{\nabla=0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dfrac{H^{1}_{c}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{3}(-\mathcal{D})\right)}{\nabla\cdot H^{1}_{c}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{2}(-\mathcal{D})\right)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{G,\mathrm{rig},c,-D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \partial\ }

Proof.

For the first two formulae, we know that both of the relevant spectral sequences have
E_{1}^{ij}=0
unless
i\geqslant 1
and
j\geqslant 2
, so
E^{i,3-i}_{\infty}=0
for
i\neq 1
, and
E_{\infty}^{12}=E_{2}^{12}=\ker(E_{1}^{12}\to E_{1}^{22})
.
∎

For the two spectral sequences over
\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}
, the results of Corollary
0.12.1.8
can be sharpened enormously by taking into account the action of the Hecke operator
U_{2}^{\prime}
. Recall that the coherent cohomology groups (both with and without
-D
) have slope decompositions for the action of
U_{2}^{\prime}
, so the slope 0 subspace is finite-dimensional and there exists an idempotent projector
e(U_{2}^{\prime})
projecting onto this subspace. Moreover, the operator
U_{2}^{\prime}
, and hence the slope 0 projector, are compatible with the morphisms in the spectral sequence.

Proposition 0.12.1.9
.

If
n\geqslant 1
and
r_{1}-r_{2}
is sufficiently large, we have

e(U_{2}^{\prime})\cdot\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\BGG_{c}(\mathcal{V}),n)=e(U_{2}^{\prime})\cdot\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),n)=0

for
i\neq 2
.

Proof.

This is a consequence of the slope estimates of
Proposition

0.11.3.1
.
∎

Corollary 0.12.1.10
.

For any
0\leqslant q\leqslant r_{2}
,
Eqs.

0.12.7a
and
0.12.7c
give isomorphisms

(0.12.8a)

\displaystyle e(U_{2}^{\prime})\cdot\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle,\mathcal{V},1+q)

\displaystyle\rTo_{\cong}e(U_{2}^{\prime})\cdot H_{c}^{2}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{1}(-\mathcal{D})\right),

(0.12.8b)

\displaystyle e(U_{2}^{\prime})\cdot\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{V},1+q)

\displaystyle\rTo_{\cong}e(U_{2}^{\prime})\cdot H_{c}^{2}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{1}\right).

Remark 0.12.1.11
.

These isomorphisms are clearly compatible under the “forget
-D
” maps on both sides.

Definition 0.12.1.12
.

Let

\tilde{\eta}^{\geqslant 1}_{\mathrm{rig},q,-D}\in\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle,\mathcal{V},1+q)

denote the preimage of the class
\eta_{\coh,-D}^{\geqslant 1}
of
Proposition

0.11.6.3
under the isomorphism of (
0.12.8a
); and let

\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\mathrm{rig},-D}=\iota\left(\tilde{\eta}^{\geqslant 1}_{\mathrm{rig},q,-D}\right)\in H^{3}_{\mathrm{rig},c}(X_{G,\Kl,0}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-D_{0}\rangle,\mathcal{V}),

where
\iota
denotes the map on cohomology induced by the inclusion of complexes

\iota:\Fil^{q}\BGG_{c}(V)\hookrightarrow\BGG_{c}(V).

Note 0.12.1.13
.

(1)

The elements
\tilde{\eta}^{\geqslant 1}_{\mathrm{rig},q,-D}
and
\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\mathrm{rig},-D}
are necessarily in the
\Pi_{\mathrm{f}}^{\prime}
-eigenspace for the prime-to-
p
Hecke operators, since these operators commute with the ordinary projector
e(U_{2}^{\prime})
.

(2)

The space
H^{3}_{\mathrm{rig},c}(X_{G,\Kl,0}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-D_{0}\rangle,\mathcal{V})
computes the compactly-supported rigid cohomology of
Y_{G,\Kl,0}
(with coefficients in the overconvergent F-isocrystal
\mathcal{V}
), and it has a Frobenius map
\varphi
.

Properties of
\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\mathrm{rig},-D}

Proposition 0.12.1.14
.

Let
P(T)=\left(1-\tfrac{T}{\alpha}\right)(1-\tfrac{T}{\beta})
, where
\alpha,\beta
are the Hecke parameters of
\Pi_{\mathrm{f}}^{\prime}
at
p
(normalised as in
Section

0.6.2
above). Then we have

P(\varphi)\cdot\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\mathrm{rig},-D}=0.

Proof.

We shall approach this problem via a series of reductions.

Firstly, we consider the chain of morphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{3}_{\mathrm{rig},c}(X_{G,\Kl,0}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-D_{0}\rangle,\mathcal{V})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle,\mathcal{V})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V}),}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\mathrm{rig},-D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\mathrm{rig},-D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\mathrm{rig}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}

where the first map is restriction from
\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}
to
\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}}
, and the second is induced by the inclusion of sheaves
\mathcal{V}(-D)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}
. We claim that both of these maps are injective on the
\Pi_{\mathrm{f}}^{\prime}
-eigenspace. Their kernels are expressed as rigid cohomology groups of other strata in
\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}
: the first is a quotient of
H^{1}_{\mathrm{rig},c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl,0}^{=1}\langle-D_{0}\rangle,\mathcal{V})
, and the second can be expressed in terms of the boundary cohomology of the compactified Kuga–Sato variety. So we conclude that
P(\varphi)\cdot\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\mathrm{rig},-D}=0
if and only if
P(\varphi)\cdot\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\mathrm{rig}}=0
.

The advantage of this reformulation is that the action of Frobenius on
H^{3}_{\mathrm{rig},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V})
can be described explicitly: it is given by the Hecke correspondence
\Phi
, since this is a lifting of the Frobenius on the special fibre. Moreover,
\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\mathrm{rig}}
has an alternative description via the Frölicher spectral sequence. The chain of morphisms above has analogues in filtered rigid cohomology, and in coherent cohomology:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\coh,-D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh,-D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{1}(-\mathcal{D}))^{\nabla=0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{N}^{1}(-\mathcal{D}))^{\nabla=0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{N}^{1})^{\nabla=0},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{3}_{\mathrm{rig},c}(X_{G,\Kl,0}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-D_{0}\rangle,\mathcal{V},1+q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle,\mathcal{V},1+q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V},1+q),}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{3}_{rig,c}(X_{G,\Kl,0}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-D_{0}\rangle,\mathcal{V})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle,\mathcal{V})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V}),}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\mathrm{rig},-D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\mathrm{rig},-D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\mathrm{rig}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{G,\mathrm{rig},c0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}

We know that
Z^{\prime}\cdot P(\Phi)\cdot\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh,-D}=0
, from which it follows that
Z^{\prime}\cdot P(\Phi)\cdot\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh}=0
and hence
Z^{\prime}\cdot P(\Phi)\cdot\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\mathrm{rig}}=0
. However, since
Z^{\prime}\cdot\Phi=U_{2}^{\prime}
, the operator
Z^{\prime}
is surjective restricted to any finite-slope
U_{2}^{\prime}
-eigenspace in
H^{3}_{\mathrm{rig},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V})
; since any such eigenspace is finite-dimensional, it is also injective, and thus we may conclude
P(\Phi)\cdot\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\mathrm{rig}}=0
.
∎

Corollary 0.12.1.15
.

The class
\tilde{\eta}^{\geqslant 1}_{\mathrm{rig},q,-D}
maps to
\eta_{\mathrm{dR},q,-D}
under the cospecialisation map.

Proof.

Note that the image of the class under the cospecialisation map lands in
\Fil^{1+q}
by construction. By Note
0.6.7.4
, it is hence sufficient to show that the unfiltered class

\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\mathrm{rig},-D}\in H^{3}_{\mathrm{rig},c}\left(X_{\Kl,0}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-D_{0}\rangle,\mathcal{V}\right)

satisfies
P(\varphi)=0
. But this is true by Propositon
0.12.1.14
.
∎

0.12.2.
Gros fp-cohomology and the Poznań spectral sequence

Gros fp-cohomology

Let
P\in 1+T\mathbf{Q}_{p}[T]
be a polynomial with constant term 1. Recall the definition of
Gros fp-cohomology
given in
Definition

0.9.2.20
above. In our present context this becomes:

Definition 0.12.2.1
.

Define the
Gros
\mathrm{fp}
-cohomology of
\mathcal{V}
over
\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}}
with
c0
-support, twist
n
and polynomial
P
, denoted
\widetilde{H}^{\bullet}_{\rigfp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V},n;P)
, to be the cohomology of the mapping fibre of the diagram

(0.12.9)

\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V},n)\rTo^{\vskip 3.01389ptP(\varphi/p^{n})\circ\iota\vskip 3.01389pt}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V}).

where
\iota
is the map on cohomology induced by the inclusion
{\mathscr{F}il}^{n}\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
. We denote the analogous group formed using the sheaves
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}(-D)
instead of
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
by
\widetilde{H}^{\bullet}_{\rigfp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V}(-D),n;P)
.

Lemma 0.12.2.2
.

For all
i\geqslant 0
, we have a surjective map

\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\rigfp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V},n;P)\rOnto\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V},n)^{P(p^{-n}\varphi)\circ\iota=0}.

Proof.

Clear from the long exact sequence associated to the mapping fibre.
∎

The Poznań spectral sequence

The definition of Gros fp-cohomology (
Definition

0.12.2.1
) also makes sense for cohomology of
\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}
with compact support. However, in the setting of
\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{G,\Kl}
we have an additional structure, since we have a lifting of the Frobenius map to the cohomology of the individual sheaves
\mathcal{N}^{i}
. This allows us to study Gros fp-cohomology via a spectral sequence, as follows.

Definition 0.12.2.3
.

We define
\mathscr{C}^{\bullet,j}_{\fp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),n;P)
to be the mapping fibre of the morphism of complexes

\mathscr{C}^{\bullet,j}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),n)\rTo^{P(\varphi/p^{n})\circ\iota}\mathscr{C}^{\bullet,j}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V})).

Thus

\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{\fp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),n;P)=H^{j}_{c0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},{\mathscr{F}il}^{n}\mathcal{N}^{i}\right)\oplus H^{j}_{c0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{N}^{i-1}\right)

with the differentials being
(x,y)\mapsto(\nabla x,P(\varphi/p^{n})\iota(x)-\nabla y)
. We shall only use this for
n\geqslant 1
, in which case one sees easily that this group is zero unless
j\in\{2,3\}
and
1\leqslant i\leqslant 4
, and the
i=0
terms vanish if
n\geqslant 1
.

Proposition 0.12.2.4
.

There is a first-quadrant spectral sequence, the
Poznań spectral sequence
, with

{}^{\Pz}E_{1}^{ij}=\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{\fp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),n;P).

The spectral sequence degenerates at
E_{3}
, and its abutment is the Gros fp-cohomology (
0.12.9
).

Proof.

Choose double complexes computing
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V},n)
and
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V},n)
respectively, in such a way that
P(\varphi/p^{n})\circ\iota
extends to a map of double complexes. Then
\widetilde{H}^{\bullet}_{\rigfp,c-a}(Y_{G,\Kl,0}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{E},n,P)
is computed by the total complex of the associated mapping fibre, i.e. by the total complex of a triple complex.
The Poznań spectral sequence is one of the spectral sequences associated to this triple complex.
∎

Coherent fp-pairs

Definition 0.12.2.5
.

(a)

We define a
coherent fp-pair
of degree
(i,j)
, twist
n
and
c0
-support to be an element of

\mathscr{Z}^{ij}_{\fp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),n;P)\coloneqq\ker\left(\mathscr{C}^{ij}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),n;P)\rTo\mathscr{C}^{i+1,j}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),n;P)\right),

i.e. a pair of elements

\left(x,y\right)\in H_{c0}^{j}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},{\mathscr{F}il}^{n}\mathcal{N}^{i}\right)\oplus H_{c0}^{j}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{N}^{i-1})

which satisfy
\nabla(x)=0
,
\nabla(y)=P(p^{-n}\varphi)\iota(x)
, where
\iota
is the map on cohomology induced by the inclusion
{\mathscr{F}il}^{n}\mathcal{N}^{i}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}^{i}
.

(b)

We define the
group of coherent fp-classes
of degree
(i,j)
, to be the
E^{ij}_{2}
-term of the Poznań spectral sequence, so it is the quotient of the group of coherent fp-pairs by the subgroup of pairs of the form

(x,y)=\left(\nabla(u),P(p^{-n}\varphi)\iota(u)-\nabla(v)\right)

for some
(u,v)\in\mathscr{C}^{i-1,j}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),n;P)
. We denote this quotient by
\mathcal{H}^{i,j}_{\fp,c0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),n;P\right)
.

Lemma 0.12.2.6
.

For any
j
and
n
there is a long exact sequence

\begin{diagram}

Proof.

This is the long exact sequence associated to the mapping fibre (
0.12.2.3
).
∎

Corollary 0.12.2.7
.

If
0\leqslant q\leqslant r_{2}
, then the spectral sequence gives rise to an isomorphism

\displaystyle\alpha_{G,\rigfp,c0}:\mathscr{Z}^{1,2}_{\fp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),1+q;P)

\displaystyle\rTo^{\cong}\mathcal{H}^{1,2}_{\fp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),1+q;P)

\displaystyle\rTo^{\cong}\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\rigfp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V},1+q;P).

Proof.

Immediate from the fact that
{}^{\Pz}E^{ij}_{r}
is supported in the range
i\geqslant 1,j\geqslant 2
.
∎

Note 0.12.2.8
.

Replacing
\BGG(\mathcal{V})
by
\DR(\mathcal{V})
, we obtain an isomorphism

(0.12.10)

\mathcal{H}^{1,2}_{\fp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\DR(\mathcal{V}),1+q;P)\rTo^{\cong}\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\rigfp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V},1+q;P)

which is compatible with the natural map

\mathcal{H}^{1,2}_{\fp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),1+q;P)\rTo\mathcal{H}^{1,2}_{\fp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\DR(\mathcal{V}),1+q;P)

arising from Proposition
0.12.1.2
(c.f. Note
0.12.1.5
).

Comparison of spectral sequences

There is a crucial compatibility between the edge maps of the Poznań spectral sequence and the Frölicher spectral sequence for (truncated) rigid cohomology:

Proposition 0.12.2.9
.

If
0\leqslant q\leqslant r_{2}
, then we have a commutative diagram

\begin{diagram}

Here, the horizontal arrows are the surjections of
Lemmas

0.12.2.6
and
0.12.2.2
, and the vertical isomorphisms are given by
Corollaries

0.12.1.8
and
0.12.2.7
.

Proof.

Clear from the construction.
∎

0.12.3.
Coherent fp-pairs from
\eta

From now on, let
0\leqslant q\leqslant r_{2}
, and let

P_{q}(T)=(1-p^{q+1}T/\alpha)(1-p^{q+1}T/\beta).

Proposition 0.12.3.1
.

There exists a unique class

\tilde{\eta}_{\rigfp,q,-D}^{\geqslant 1}\in\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\rigfp,c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle,\mathcal{V},1+q;P_{q})

which lies in the
\Pi_{\mathrm{f}}^{\prime}
-eigenspace for the spherical Hecke operators, and whose image in
\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle,\mathcal{V},1+q)
is
\tilde{\eta}_{\mathrm{rig},q,-D}^{\geqslant 1}
.

Note 0.12.3.2
.

Note that the class
\tilde{\eta}_{\rigfp,q,-D}^{\geqslant 1}
, and the group in which it lies, are actually independent of
q
in this range (but the different values of
q
correspond, formally, to different twists of the motive).

Proof.

From
Proposition

0.12.1.14
we know that

\tilde{\eta}_{\mathrm{rig},q,-D}^{\geqslant 1}\in\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle,\mathcal{V},1+q)^{P_{n}(p^{-(1+q)}\varphi)\circ\iota=0}.

It follows that
\tilde{\eta}_{\mathrm{rig},q,-D}^{\geqslant 1}
is in the image of the map

\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\rigfp,c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle,\mathcal{V},1+q;P_{q})\to\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\mathrm{dR},c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle,\mathcal{V},1+q),

and the kernel of this map is a quotient of
H^{2}_{\mathrm{rig},c}(X_{G,\Kl,0}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-D_{0}\rangle,\mathcal{V})
, which has zero
\Pi_{\mathrm{f}}^{\prime}
-eigenspace, so there is a unique
\Pi_{\mathrm{f}}^{\prime}
-equivariant lift of
\tilde{\eta}_{\mathrm{rig},q,-D}^{\geqslant 1}
to a class
in
\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\rigfp,c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle,\mathcal{V},1+q;P_{q})
.
∎

Corollary 0.12.3.3
.

The class
\tilde{\eta}_{\rigfp,q,-D}^{\geqslant 1}
is sent to
\eta_{\rigfp,q,-D}^{\geqslant 1}
under the corestriction map.

Definition 0.12.3.4
.

Define

\tilde{\eta}_{\rigfp,q}^{\mathrm{ord}}\in\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\rigfp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V},1+q;P_{q})

to be the image of
\tilde{\eta}_{\rigfp,q,-D}^{\geqslant 1}
under restriction to
\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}
and forgetting
-D
.

Notation 0.12.3.5
.

Write
\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}^{[t_{1},t_{2}]}_{\rigsyn,\underline{\Phi},\mathrm{ord}}
for the image of
\mathrm{Eis}^{[t_{1},t_{2}]}_{\rigsyn,\underline{\Phi},\mathrm{ord}}
in
\tilde{H}^{2}_{\rigsyn}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta},2)
under the specialisation map defined in Remark
0.9.2.22
.

By Remark
0.9.2.22
, we obtain the following result:

Corollary 0.12.3.6
.

Then

\left\langle\mathrm{Eis}^{[t_{1},t_{2}]}_{\rigsyn,\underline{\Phi},\mathrm{ord}},\,(\iota_{\Delta}^{[t_{1},t_{2}]})^{*}(\eta_{\rigfp,q,-D}^{\geqslant 1}|_{Y^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\Kl}})\right\rangle_{\rigfp,Y^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}}\\
=\left\langle\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}^{[t_{1},t_{2}]}_{\rigsyn,\underline{\Phi},\mathrm{ord}},\,(\iota_{\Delta}^{[t_{1},t_{2}]})^{*}(\tilde{\eta}_{\rigfp,q,-D}^{\geqslant 1}|_{Y^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\Kl}})\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\rigfp},Y^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}}.

In Section
0.16
, we will evaluate this pairing in terms of coherent cohomology.

Note 0.12.3.7
.

By
Corollary

0.12.2.7
, we have an isomorphism

\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\rigfp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V},1+q;P_{q})\cong\mathscr{Z}^{1,2}_{\fp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\BGG(\mathcal{V}),1+q;P_{q}).

Thus
\tilde{\eta}_{\rigfp,q}^{\mathrm{ord}}
is represented by a uniquely-determined coherent fp-pair, which clearly has the form
\left(\eta_{\coh}^{\mathrm{ord}},\zeta\right)
for some class

(0.12.11)

\zeta\in H^{2}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{N}^{0})\quad\text{such that}\quad P_{1+q}(\Phi_{1+q})\,\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh}=\nabla\zeta.

Note 0.12.3.8
.

Equation (
0.12.11
) does not determine
\zeta
uniquely: it is only unique modulo

H^{2}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{N}^{0})^{\nabla=0}\cong H^{2}_{\mathrm{dR},c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V}).

However, we know that this group has zero
\Pi_{\mathrm{f}}^{\prime}
-eigenspace, so there is a unique
\Pi_{\mathrm{f}}^{\prime}
-isotypical lifting.

The following proposition is crucial for the evaluation of the regulator:

Proposition 0.12.3.9
.

The element
\zeta
has the following properties:

•

Z^{\prime}\cdot\zeta=0
,

•

U_{2}^{\prime}\cdot\zeta=\lambda\zeta
, where
\lambda=\tfrac{\alpha\beta}{p^{(r_{2}+1)}}
is the unit eigenvalue of
U_{2}^{\prime}
on
\Pi_{p}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We know that
P(\Phi)\cdot\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh}
is in the kernel of the operators
Z^{\prime}
and
(U_{2}^{\prime}-\lambda)
. Hence
(U^{\prime}_{2}-\lambda)\cdot\zeta
and
Z^{\prime}\cdot\zeta
lie in the group
H^{2}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{N}^{0})^{\nabla=0}
. Moreover, they are both in the
\Pi_{\mathrm{f}}^{\prime}
-eigenspace for the spherical Hecke algebra. Since this eigenspace is zero, we conclude that
\zeta
is in the kernel of
Z^{\prime}
and of
(U_{2}^{\prime}-\lambda)
.
∎

0.12.4.
Lifting to the de Rham sheaves

Definition 0.12.4.1
.

•

Define
\breve{\zeta}
to be the image of
\zeta
in
H^{2}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega_{G}^{0}\langle D\rangle)
.

•

For
0\leqslant q\leqslant r_{2}
, define
\breve{\eta}^{\geqslant 1}_{\coh,q,-D}
to be the image of
\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\coh,-D}
under the composition of maps

H^{2}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{1}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle)\rTo H^{2}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},{\mathscr{F}il}^{r_{2}}\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega_{G}^{1}\langle-D\rangle)\rTo H^{2}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},{\mathscr{F}il}^{q}\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega_{G}^{1}\langle-D\rangle),

where the first map is given by the inclusion of complexes in Proposition
0.12.1.2
, and the second map is induced from the natural inclusion of sheaves.

•

Write
\breve{\eta}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh,q}
for the image of
\breve{\eta}^{\geqslant 1}_{\coh,q,-D}|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{G,\Kl}}
in
H^{2}_{c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},{\mathscr{F}il}^{q}\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega_{G}^{1}\langle D\rangle)
.

Lemma 0.12.4.2
.

The class
\breve{\eta}^{\geqslant 1}_{\coh,q,-D}
maps to
{\eta}^{\geqslant 1}_{\coh,-D}
under the natural map induced from the projection

{\mathscr{F}il}^{r_{2}}\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega_{G}^{1}\langle-D\rangle)\rTo\mathcal{N}^{1}.

Proof.

It is immediate from Propositon
0.12.1.2
that the image of
\mathcal{N}^{1}
in the full de Rham complex is contained in
{\mathscr{F}il}^{r_{2}}\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega_{G}^{1}
. In order to prove the result, it is hence sufficient to show that composition of the inclusion and the projection map is the identity on
\mathcal{N}^{1}
. But this follows from the results in
[
FC90
, Ch. VI, §6]
.
∎

The following two lemmas are direct consequences of the corresponding results for
\eta^{\geqslant 1}_{\coh,-D}
and
\zeta
(Propositions
0.11.6.3
and
0.12.3.9
), using the the inclusion of the dual BGG complex into the de Rham complex is Hecke equivariant.

Lemma 0.12.4.3
.

•

The operator
U^{\prime}_{\Kl,2}
acts on
\breve{\eta}^{\geqslant 1}_{\coh,q,-D}
as multiplication by
\frac{\alpha\beta}{p^{r_{2}+1}}
.

•

The operator
U^{\prime}_{\Kl,1}
acts on
\breve{\eta}^{\geqslant 1}_{\coh,q,-D}
as multiplication by
\alpha+\beta
.

•

The spherical Hecke algebra acts via the system of eigenvalues associated to
\Pi^{\prime}
.

Lemma 0.12.4.4
.

The element
\breve{\zeta}
has the following properties:

•

Z^{\prime}\cdot\breve{\zeta}=0
,

•

U_{2}^{\prime}\cdot\breve{\zeta}=\lambda\breve{\zeta}
, where
\lambda=\tfrac{\alpha\beta}{p^{(r_{2}+1)}}
is the unit eigenvalue of
U_{2}^{\prime}
on
\Pi_{p}^{\prime}
.

Proposition 0.12.4.5
.

The classes
\breve{\zeta}
and
\breve{\eta}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh}
satisfy

\nabla\breve{\eta}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh,q}=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad P_{q}(\Phi_{1+q})\,\breve{\eta}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh,q}=\nabla\breve{\zeta}

and hence give rise to a class in
\mathcal{H}^{1,2}_{\fp,c0}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\DR(\mathcal{V}),1+q;P_{q})
. Moreover, this class maps to
\tilde{\eta}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\rigfp,q}
under the isomorphism (
0.12.10
).

Proof.

Immediate.
∎

0.12.5.
A Hecke operator identity

The reason why we care about Proposition
0.12.3.9
is the following result, comparing constructions on
G
and on
H
. Recall the embedding

\iota_{\Delta}:\mathcal{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}}\to\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}}

constructed in Section
0.10.3
, and recall that its image is also closed in
\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}
.

Proposition 0.12.5.1
.

We have the following identity of correspondences
\mathcal{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}}\rightrightarrows\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}}
:

U_{2}^{\prime}\circ\iota_{\Delta}\circ(U_{p}\boxtimes U_{p})=p\langle p\rangle Z^{\prime}\circ\iota_{\Delta}.

Note 0.12.5.2
.

Correspondences act contravariantly on cohomology, so this means that

(U_{p}\boxtimes U_{p})\circ\iota_{\Delta}^{*}\circ U_{2}^{\prime}=\iota_{\Delta}^{*}\circ p\langle p\rangle Z^{\prime}

as maps
H^{*}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}})\to H^{*}(\mathcal{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}})
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{Y}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}}
is open in
\mathcal{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}}
, it suffices to prove the identity over this open subset.

We recall the moduli-space description of the varieties and correspondences involved. A point of
\mathcal{Y}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}}
(over some
p
-adic field
L
) corresponds to a triple
(E_{1},E_{2},\alpha)
, where
E_{i}
are elliptic curves over
L
with good ordinary reduction, and
\alpha
is an isomorphism
\hat{E}_{1}[p]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\hat{E}_{2}[p]
. The operator
U_{p}\boxtimes U_{p}
maps
(E_{1},E_{2},\alpha)
to the formal sum
\sum_{J_{1},J_{2}}(E_{1}/J_{1},E_{2}/J_{2},\bar{\alpha})
where
J_{i}
vary over cyclic
p
-subgroups of
E_{i}
distinct from
\hat{E}_{i}[p]
, and
\bar{\alpha}
is the ensuing isomorphism

\widehat{E_{1}/J_{1}}[p]\lTo_{\cong}\hat{E}_{1}[p]\rTo^{\alpha}_{\cong}\hat{E}_{2}[p]\rTo_{\cong}\widehat{E_{2}/J_{2}}[p].

Concretely, if
e_{1},f_{1}
denotes a choice of basis of
T_{p}E_{1}
, and
e_{2},f_{2}
of
T_{p}E_{2}
, giving isomorphisms
E_{i}[p^{\infty}]\cong(\mathbf{Q}_{p}/\mathbf{Z}_{p})^{2}
, and we assume that
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
span the Tate modules of the formal groups
T_{p}\hat{E}_{i}
, then
J_{1}
has to be one of the groups
\langle\tfrac{f_{1}+a_{1}e_{1}}{p}\rangle
for
0\leqslant a_{1}\leqslant p-1
, and similarly
J_{2}
. We can and do assume that
\alpha(e_{1})=e_{2}
.

Meanwhile, points of
\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}}
correspond to pairs
(A,C)
where
A
is an abelian surface and
C\subset\hat{A}[p]
is a cyclic
p
-subgroup (again with some prime-to-
p
level structure being ignored). The map
\iota_{\Delta}
maps
(E_{1},E_{2},\alpha)
to
(E_{1}\oplus E_{2},C)
where
C\subset(\hat{E}_{1}\oplus\hat{E}_{2})[p]
is the subgroup of points of the form
(x,\alpha(x))
.

Finally, the Hecke correspondences
Z^{\prime}
,
U_{2}^{\prime}
and
\langle p\rangle
are given as follows. Let
P=(A,C)
be a point of
\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}}
.

•

The correspondence
Z^{\prime}
is given by

(A,C)\mapsto\sum_{J}\sum_{\tilde{C}}(A/J,\tilde{C}\bmod J),

where
J
varies over isotropic
(p,p)
-subgroups such that
J\cap\hat{A}[p]=C
, and
\tilde{C}
varies over cyclic
p^{2}
-subgroups of
\widehat{A/J}[p]
such that
p\tilde{C}=C
. (Note that there are
p
choices of
J
, and
p
choices of the subgroup
\tilde{C}
, so this is a correspondence of degree
p^{2}
.)

•

For the correspondence
U_{2}^{\prime}
, let
J_{0}
be the subgroup
(p^{-1}C\cap\hat{A})+C^{\perp}
; this has invariants
(p^{2},p,p)
and is isotropic in the sense that
pJ_{0}
and
J_{0}[p]
are orthogonal complements inside
A[p]
. Then
U_{2}^{\prime}
is given by

(A,C)\mapsto\sum_{\tilde{C}}(A/J_{0},(p^{-1}\tilde{C}\cap\hat{A})\bmod J_{0}),

where
\tilde{C}
again varies over liftings of
C
to a cyclic
p^{2}
-subgroup of
\hat{A}
.

•

The correspondence
\langle p\rangle
sends
(A,C)
to itself, but acts on the prime-to-
p
level structure by multiplying it by
p
.

We now consider composing these operations. We choose a point
P=(E_{1},E_{2},\alpha)
and fix coordinates on the
E_{i}
, as above. Let
(A,C)=\iota_{1}(P)=(E_{1}\oplus E_{2},\langle\tfrac{e_{1}+e_{2}}{p}\rangle)
; and let
(A^{\prime},C^{\prime})=\iota_{\Delta}(P^{\prime})
where
P^{\prime}
is one of the points in the 0-cycle
(U_{p},U_{p})\cdot P
, corresponding to a choice of
a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathbf{Z}/p
; thus we have
A^{\prime}=A/\langle f_{1}^{\prime},f_{2}^{\prime}\rangle
, where
f_{i}^{\prime}=\tfrac{f_{i}+a_{i}e_{i}}{p}
. Thus
(e_{1},e_{2},f_{1}^{\prime},f_{2}^{\prime})
form a basis of
T_{p}A^{\prime}
, regarded as a lattice in
V=T_{p}A\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{p}
(strictly containing
T_{p}A
itself), and
C^{\prime}
is the image of
C
, generated by
\tfrac{e_{1}+e_{2}}{p}
as a subgroup of
V/T_{p}A^{\prime}
.

We now compute the canonical
(p^{2},p,p)
-subgroup
J_{0}
of
A^{\prime}
: it is uniquely determined by
pJ_{0}=C=\langle\tfrac{e_{1}+e_{2}}{p}\rangle
and
J_{0}[p]=C^{\perp}=\langle\tfrac{e_{1}}{p},\tfrac{e_{2}}{p},\tfrac{f_{1}^{\prime}-f_{2}^{\prime}}{p}\rangle
, from which we easily compute that
J_{0}
is generated by
\langle\tfrac{e_{1}+e_{2}}{p^{2}},\tfrac{e_{1}}{p},\tfrac{f_{1}^{\prime}-f_{2}^{\prime}}{p}\rangle
as a subgroup of
A^{\prime}[p^{\infty}]=V/T_{p}A^{\prime}
. Note that this subgroup contains the image of
A[p]
. Thus the isogeny
A\to A^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}/J_{0}
is the composite of multiplication by
p
on
A
(which gives the factor
\langle p\rangle
) and quotient by the subgroup
K=\langle\tfrac{e_{1}+e_{2}}{p},\tfrac{f_{1}-f_{2}+a_{1}e_{1}-a_{2}e_{2}}{p}\rangle
. So the image of
(A^{\prime},C^{\prime})
under
U_{2}^{\prime}
is given by
\sum_{C^{\prime}}(A/K,C^{\prime}\bmod K)
, where
C^{\prime}
varies over multiplicative
p^{2}
-subgroups of
A
lifting
C
. Note that this is the same as the inner sum of
Z^{\prime}\cdot(A,C)
when we take the subgroup
J
to be our
K
.

To conclude the proof, it suffices to note that as
(a_{1},a_{2})
vary, the subgroup
K
hits every one of the groups
J
in the outer sum defining
Z^{\prime}\cdot(A,C)
, and each such
J
occurs
p
times (since
K
only depends on
a_{1}-a_{2}\bmod p
).
∎

Proposition
0.12.5.1
has the following immediate consequence, which will be crucial in the regulator evaluation (c.f. Section
0.16
):

Corollary 0.12.5.3
.

Let
z\in H^{2}_{c0}(X_{\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega_{G}^{0})
, and assume that
z\in\ker(Z^{\prime})
and
z
is a
U_{2}^{\prime}
-eigenvector with non-zero eigenvalue. Then

\iota_{\Delta}^{*}(z)\in\ker(U_{p}\boxtimes U_{p}).

Note 0.12.5.4
.

By Lemma
0.12.4.4
, it hence follows that
\iota_{\Delta}^{*}(\breve{\zeta})\in\ker(U_{p}\boxtimes U_{p})
.

0.13.
fp-cohomology and coherent fp-pairs for
H

0.13.1.
The Poznań spectral sequence

Similar considerations apply to
\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}
, with or without compact support. Let
W
be an algebraic representation of
H
, and write
\mathcal{W}
for the corresponding coherent sheaf on
\mathcal{X}_{\Delta}
. Let
Q\in\mathbf{Q}_{p}[t]
have constant coefficient
1
, and let
n\geqslant 0
. We can then consider the Gros-fp cohomology

\widetilde{H}^{\bullet}_{\rigfp,\star}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\Delta}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle,\mathcal{W},n;Q),

where
\star\in\{\varnothing,c\}
and
\diamondsuit\in\{\varnothing,-\mathcal{D}_{\Delta}\}
(c.f.
Definition

0.12.2.1
).

Recall that if
Q(p^{-1})\neq 0
, we define the trace map

\widetilde{H}^{5}_{\rigfp,c}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta},\mathbf{Q}_{p},3;Q)\to\mathbf{Q}_{p}

as
\tfrac{1}{Q(p^{-1})}
times the trace map on rigid cohomology.

Remark 0.13.1.1
.

As usual, the factor
\tfrac{1}{Q(p^{-1})}
serves to make the trace maps compatible with the natural maps of complexes
\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rigfp,c}(-;Q)\to\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rigfp,c}(-;Q^{\prime})
for polynomials
Q\mid Q^{\prime}
. (This map acts as
(Q^{\prime}/Q)(p^{-n}\varphi)
on the rigid complex, with
n=3
; but
\varphi=p^{2}
on the top-degree cohomology, hence
Q(p^{-1})
is the correct normalising factor.)

Definition 0.13.1.2

(cf.
Definition

0.12.2.3
)
.

For
j,n\geqslant 0
,
\star\in\{\emptyset,c\}
and
\diamondsuit\in\{\varnothing,-\mathcal{D}_{\Delta}\}
, we define the complex

\mathscr{C}^{\bullet,j}_{\star}(X_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle,\mathcal{W},n;Q)

with terms

\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{\star}(X_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle,\mathcal{W},n;Q)=H^{j}_{\star}(\mathcal{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}},{\mathscr{F}il}^{n-i}\mathcal{W}\otimes\Omega_{\Delta}^{i}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle)\oplus H^{j}_{\star}(\mathcal{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}},\mathcal{W}\otimes\Omega_{\Delta}^{i-1}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle)

and differentials

(x,y)\mapsto\left(\nabla x,\,Q(\varphi_{H}^{*}/p^{n})\iota(x)-\nabla y\right).

Proposition 0.13.1.3
.

For
\star\in\{\varnothing,c\}
and
\diamondsuit\in\{\varnothing,-\mathcal{D}_{\Delta}\}
, we have the
Poznań spectral sequence

{}^{\Pz}E_{1}^{ij}=\mathscr{C}^{i,j}_{\star}(X_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle,\mathcal{W},n;Q)\Rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{i+j}_{\rigfp,\star}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle,\mathcal{W},n;Q).

Proof.

Analogous to the proof of Proposition
0.12.2.4

∎

We define the group of coherent fp-classes, denoted
H^{i,j}_{\star}(X_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle,\mathcal{W},n;Q)
, analogously to
Definition

0.12.2.5
.

Corollary 0.13.1.4
.

The Poznań spectral sequence gives rise to isomorphisms

\displaystyle\alpha_{\Delta}:

\displaystyle H^{i,0}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle,\mathcal{W},n;Q)\rTo_{\cong}\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\rigfp}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle,\mathcal{W},n;Q),

\displaystyle\alpha_{\Delta,c}:

\displaystyle H_{c}^{i,2}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle,\mathcal{W},n;Q)\rTo_{\cong}\widetilde{H}^{i+2}_{\rigfp,c}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle,\mathcal{W},n;Q).

Proof.

Easy computation, using that since
\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\Delta}
is affinoid, we have

\displaystyle H^{i}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle,\mathcal{W})=0

for
i\neq 0
,

\displaystyle H^{i}_{c}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle,\mathcal{W})=0

\displaystyle\qquad\text{for $i\neq 2$}.

∎

(Note that this holds for both
\diamondsuit=\varnothing

and

\diamondsuit=-\mathcal{D}_{\GL_{2}}
, in contrast to the situation for
G
.)

Note 0.13.1.5
.

In particular, if
n\geqslant 3
we have

\displaystyle H_{c}^{3,2}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}\langle-\mathcal{D}_{\Delta}\rangle,\mathbf{Q}_{p},n;Q)

\displaystyle\rTo^{\cong}\widetilde{H}^{5}_{\rigfp,c}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}\langle-\mathcal{D}_{\Delta}\rangle,\mathbf{Q}_{p},n;Q)

\displaystyle\cong\mathbf{Q}_{p}.

The Frobenius operator
\varphi_{H}^{*}
acts on
\widetilde{H}^{5}_{\rigfp,c}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}\langle-\mathcal{D}_{\Delta}\rangle,\mathbf{Q}_{p},n;Q)
as multiplication by
p^{2}
.

Note 0.13.1.6
.

Similarly, let
U
be an algebraic representation of
\GL_{2}
, and write
\mathcal{U}
for the corresponding coherent sheaf on
\mathcal{X}_{\GL_{2}}
. Let
\diamondsuit\in\{\varnothing,-\mathcal{D}_{\GL_{2}}\}
. Then the Poznań spectral sequence gives rise to an isomorphism

(0.13.1)

\alpha_{\GL_{2}}:H^{i,0}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\GL_{2}}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle,\mathcal{U},n;Q)\rTo_{\cong}\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\rigfp}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\GL_{2}}\langle\diamondsuit\rangle,\mathcal{U};Q).

0.13.2.
Compatibility with cup products

Lemma 0.13.2.1
.

Using the same formalism as
[
Bes12
, §2]
, we can construct a cup product

H^{i,0}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}\langle-\mathcal{D}_{\Delta}\rangle,\mathcal{W},m;P)\times H_{c}^{j,2}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta},\mathcal{W}^{\vee},n;Q)\rTo^{\cup}H_{c}^{i+j,2}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta}\langle-\mathcal{D}_{\Delta}\rangle,\mathbf{Q}_{p},m+n;P\star Q)

which is compatible under the isomorphisms from Corollary
0.13.1.4
with the cup product in Gros-fp cohomology.

Proof.

Standard check.
∎

Note 0.13.2.2
.

If
m+n\geqslant 3
and
i+j=3
, then we obtain a pairing

(0.13.2)

\langle\quad,\quad\rangle_{H}:\,H^{i,0}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta},\mathcal{W}(-\mathcal{D}_{\Delta}),m;P)\times H_{c}^{j,2}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta},\mathcal{W}^{\vee},n;Q)\rTo\mathbf{Q}_{p}.

0.14.
Coherent versus de Rham pullbacks

0.14.1.
Algebraic representations of
G
and
H

We can identify the representation
\Sym^{k}
of
\GL_{2}
with the space of polynomial functions on
\GL_{2}
satisfying

f\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&0\\
\star&\star\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cdot g\right)=a^{k}f(g).

If
v
and
w
are the functions
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x&y\\
\star&\star\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mapsto x
and
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x&y\\
\star&\star\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mapsto y
, then
\{v^{k-i}w^{i}:0\leqslant i\leqslant k\}
is the standard basis of
\Sym^{k}
, with
v^{k}
being the highest-weight vector. Note that if
X_{21}
denotes the generator
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
of the Lie algebra, then we have

(X_{21})^{i}\cdot v^{k}=\tfrac{k!}{(k-i)!}v^{k-i}w^{i}.

Now let us return to the setting where
V_{G}=V_{G}(r_{1},r_{2};r_{1}+r_{2})
for some
r_{1}\geqslant r_{2}\geqslant 0
, and
V_{H}=V_{H}(t_{1},t_{2};t_{1}+t_{2})
, where
(t_{1},t_{2})=(r_{1}-q-r,r_{2}-q+r)
for some
0\leqslant q\leqslant r_{2}
,
0\leqslant r\leqslant r_{1}-r_{2}
as per our running conventions.

Since the representation
V_{H}(t_{1},t_{2};t_{1}+t_{2})
of
H
is the exterior product
\Sym^{t_{1}}\boxtimes\Sym^{t_{2}}
, we thus have a weight-vector basis
\{v^{t_{1}-i_{1}}w^{i_{1}}\boxtimes v^{t_{2}-i_{2}}w^{i_{2}}:0\leqslant i_{n}\leqslant t_{n}\}
of this representation, realised as a space of
\overline{N}_{H}
-invariant functions on
H
.

We can similarly model
V_{G}(r_{1},r_{2})
as the space of
f\in\mathcal{O}(\overline{N}_{B}\backslash G)
which transform via the character
\lambda(r_{1},r_{2};r_{1}+r_{2})
under left-translation by
T
. The standard basis vectors
v_{1},\dots,v_{4}
of the 4-dimensional representation
V(1,0)
thus correspond to the functions sending
g\in G
to the four entries of its first row. A choice of highest-weight vector
w
of
V(1,1)
is given by
g\mapsto\left|\begin{smallmatrix}g_{11}&g_{12}\\
g_{21}&g_{22}\end{smallmatrix}\right|
, and the vector
w^{\prime}=Z\cdot w
is
g\mapsto\left|\begin{smallmatrix}g_{13}&g_{14}\\
g_{23}&g_{24}\end{smallmatrix}\right|
.

In
[
LSZ16
]
§4.3 we described a specific choice of morphism

\br^{[q,r]}:V_{H}\otimes\sideset{}{{}^{q}}{\det}\to V_{G}

given by mapping the highest-weight vector
v^{t_{1}}\boxtimes v^{t_{2}}
of
V_{H}
to the vector
v^{[q,r]}\in V_{G}
(denoted
v^{[a,b,q,r]}
in
op.cit.
) defined by

w^{r_{2}-q}\cdot(w^{\prime})^{q}\cdot v_{1}^{r_{1}-r_{2}-r}\cdot v_{2}^{r}

where the products are taken in
\mathcal{O}(\overline{N}_{B}\backslash G)
(the “Cartan product” construction).

Note 0.14.1.1
.

It is important to note that the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
acts on
\mathcal{O}(G)
by derivations, so for
X\in\mathfrak{g}
we have the Leibniz rule

X^{n}\cdot(f_{1}\times\dots\times f_{m})=\sum_{u_{1}+\dots+u_{m}=n}\binom{n}{u_{1},\dots,u_{m}}(X^{u_{1}}\cdot f_{1})\dots(X^{u_{m}}\cdot f_{m}).

In particular,
X^{n}\cdot f^{n}=n!(X\cdot f)^{n}
.

Lemma 0.14.1.2
.

Consider the vector
v^{t_{1}-t}w^{t}\boxtimes v^{t_{2}}\in V_{H}
, where
t=r_{2}-q
. The image of this vector under
\br^{[q,r]}
is in
\ker(X_{12}^{n+1})-\ker(X_{12}^{n})
, where
X_{12}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\\
&&0&-1\\
&&0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathfrak{g}
and
n=2r_{2}-q+r
. We have

\br^{[q,r]}\left(v^{t_{1}-t}w^{t}\boxtimes v^{t_{2}}\right)=\frac{1}{\binom{t_{1}}{t}}(w^{\prime\prime})^{t}(w^{\prime})^{q}v_{1}^{r_{1}-r_{2}-r}v_{2}^{r}\pmod{\ker(X_{12}^{n-1})}.

where
w^{\prime\prime}=X_{41}\cdot w=\left(g\mapsto\left|\begin{smallmatrix}g_{14}&g_{12}\\
g_{24}&g_{22}\end{smallmatrix}\right|\right)
spans the
(-1,1)
weight space of
V_{G}(1,1)
.

Proof.

We have
v^{t_{1}-t}w^{t}\boxtimes v^{t_{2}}=\tfrac{(t_{1}-t)!}{t_{1}!}X_{41}^{t}\cdot v^{t_{1}}\boxtimes v^{t_{2}}
(identifying
X_{41}
with an element of
\mathfrak{h}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}
). So we have

\br^{[q,r]}\left(v^{t_{1}-t}w^{t}\boxtimes v^{t_{2}}\right)=\tfrac{(t_{1}-t)!}{t_{1}!}X_{41}^{t}\cdot v^{[q,r]}.

We now compute how
X_{41}
acts on the four vectors used in the definition of
v^{[q,r]}
: it maps
v_{1}
to
v_{4}
and kills the other
v_{i}
; it sends
w
to
w^{\prime\prime}
, and it kills
w^{\prime}
and
w^{\prime\prime}
. So
X_{41}^{t}\cdot v^{[q,r]}
is a sum of terms of the form

w^{t-\alpha}\ (w^{\prime\prime})^{\alpha}\ (w^{\prime})^{q}\ v_{1}^{b-r-\beta}\ v_{2}^{r}\ v_{4}^{\beta}

where
\alpha+\beta=t
; and the term for
\alpha=t,\beta=0
has coefficient
t!
.

We now consider how
X_{12}
acts on this element. One checks that
X_{12}
acts on
V_{G}(1,0)
by
v_{2}\mapsto v_{1}\mapsto 0
,
v_{4}\mapsto-v_{3}\mapsto 0
; and on
V_{G}(1,1)
by
w^{\prime\prime}\mapsto-w^{\prime}
, ,
w^{\prime}\mapsto-2w_{-}
,
w_{-}\mapsto 0
,
w\mapsto 0
where
w_{-}=X_{32}\cdot w=\left(g\mapsto\left|\begin{smallmatrix}g_{11}&g_{13}\\
g_{21}&g_{23}\end{smallmatrix}\right|\right)
spans the
(1,-1)
weight space of
V(1,1)
. It follows that the number of applications of
X_{12}
needed to kill the above element is exactly
2\alpha+q+\beta+r+1
. Since
\alpha+\beta
is fixed, the last term to be annihilated is the one for
\alpha=t,\beta=0
.
∎

We now consider the image of
\br^{[q,r]}\left(v^{t_{1}-t}w^{t}\boxtimes v^{t_{2}}\right)
in the graded pieces of the
P_{\Sieg}
-stable filtration on
V_{G}
given by eigenspaces for
Z(M_{\Sieg})
as in
[
LPSZ19
, Definition 6.1]
. Note that we have
\br^{[q,r]}\left(v^{t_{1}-t}w^{t}\boxtimes v^{t_{2}}\right)\in\Fil^{r_{1}}V_{G}
. Moreover, although the representation
\Gr^{r_{1}}V_{G}
is far from being irreducible, it is semi-simple and has a unique direct summand of highest highest weight, isomorphic to
W_{G}(r_{1},-r_{2};r_{1}+r_{2})
.

Since
M_{\Sieg}\cap\operatorname{Sp}_{4}
is isomorphic to
\GL_{2}
, via
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&\\
&\star\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mapsto A
, we can identify
W_{G}(r_{1},-r_{2};r_{1}+r_{2})
with the representation
\Sym^{r_{1}+r_{2}}\otimes\det^{-r_{2}}
of
\GL_{2}
, so it has a canonical basis
v^{(r_{1}+r_{2}-i)}w^{i}
for
0\leqslant i\leqslant r_{1}+r_{2}
. We normalise the projection
\Gr^{r_{1}}V_{G}\twoheadrightarrow W_{G}(r_{1},-r_{2};r_{1}+r_{2})
to send
v_{1}^{r_{1}-r_{2}}(w_{-})^{r_{2}}
to
v^{r_{1}+r_{2}}
.

Proposition 0.14.1.3
.

The image of
\br^{[q,r]}\left(v^{t_{1}-t}w^{t}\boxtimes v^{t_{2}}\right)\in\Fil^{r_{1}}V_{G}
under projection to
W_{G}(r_{1},-r_{2};r_{1}+r_{2})
is given by

\frac{(-2)^{q}}{\binom{t_{1}}{t}}\cdot v^{r_{1}+r_{2}-n}w^{n},\quad n=2r_{2}-q+r.

Similarly, the image of
\br^{[q,r]}\left(v^{t_{1}}\boxtimes v^{t_{2}-t}w^{t}\right)
is given by

\frac{(-2)^{q}}{\binom{t_{2}}{t}}v^{r_{1}+r_{2}-m}w^{m},m=q+r.

Proof.

Letting
X=X_{12}
for brevity, and recalling that
n=q+r+2t
, we have

X^{n}\cdot(w^{\prime\prime})^{t}(w^{\prime})^{q}v_{1}^{b-r}v_{2}^{r}=\frac{n!}{(2t)!q!r!}(X^{2t}\cdot(w^{\prime\prime})^{t})\times(X^{q}\cdot(w^{\prime})^{q})\times v_{1}^{r_{1}-r_{2}-r}\times(X^{r}\cdot v_{2}^{r})

by the Leibniz rule, with all other terms being 0. Since
X^{2}w^{\prime}=0
, we have
X^{q}\cdot(w^{\prime})^{q}=q!(X\cdot w^{\prime})^{q}=(-2)^{q}q!(w_{-})^{q}
, and similarly
X^{r}\cdot v_{2}^{r}=r!v_{1}^{r}
. The term
X^{2t}\cdot(w^{\prime\prime})^{t}
is a little more fiddly to evaluate; we conclude that

X^{2t}\cdot(w^{\prime\prime})^{t}=\binom{2t}{2,\dots,2}(X^{2}\cdot w^{\prime\prime})^{t}=\frac{(2t)!}{2^{t}}(2w_{-})^{t},

so the conclusion is that

X^{2t+q+r}\cdot(w^{\prime\prime})^{t}(w^{\prime})^{q}v_{1}^{b-r}v_{2}^{r}=(-2)^{q}n!v_{1}^{r_{1}-r_{2}}(w_{-})^{r_{2}}.

On the other hand, the unique vector in the standard basis of
W_{G}(r_{1},-r_{2};r_{1}+r_{2})
having the same weight as
\br^{[q,r]}\left(v^{t_{1}-t}w^{t}\boxtimes v^{t_{2}}\right)
is
v^{r_{1}+r_{2}-n}w^{n}
, whose image under
X_{12}^{n}
is
n!v^{r_{1}+r_{2}}
. Hence the factor
(-2)^{q}
.
∎

Let us now perform a similar computation for
v^{t_{1}}\boxtimes v^{t_{2}-t}w^{t}\in V_{H}
. The image of this in
V_{G}
is clearly
\tfrac{(t_{2}-t)!}{t_{2}!}X_{32}^{t}\cdot v^{[q,r]}
and we compute that this is equal to

\tfrac{1}{\binom{t_{2}}{t}}(w_{-})^{t}v_{1}^{b-r}(w^{\prime})^{q}v_{2}^{r}

plus other terms killed by lower powers of
X_{12}
. Acting on this by
X_{12}^{q+r}
gives
(-2)^{q}(q+r)!v_{1}^{r_{1}-r_{2}}(w_{-})^{r_{2}}
, so its image in
W_{G}(r_{1},-r_{2};r_{1}+r_{2})
has to be

\frac{(-2)^{q}}{\binom{t_{2}}{t}}v^{r_{1}+r_{2}-m}w^{m},m=q+r.

Remark 0.14.1.4
.

Compare
[
LSZ17
, Theorem 9.6.4]
. With the benefit of hindsight, one can observe that it would have been better to define
v^{[q,r]}
to be
\tfrac{1}{(-2)^{q}}
times its present definition; this would simultaneously kill the error terms
(-2)^{q}
both here and in
op.cit.
.

0.14.2.
Unit-root splittings

Now let us consider the following construction. Our choice of embedding
V_{H}\otimes\det^{q}\hookrightarrow V_{G}
is strictly compatible with the filtrations, and hence gives rise to a pushforward map

H^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}},\frac{\Fil^{m}V_{H}}{\Fil^{n}V_{H}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{H}(-D)\right)\to H^{1}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\frac{\Fil^{m+q}V_{G}}{\Fil^{n+q}V_{G}}\otimes\Omega^{2}_{G}(-D)\right)

for any
m\leqslant n
, and dually a pullback map

H^{2}_{c}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\frac{\Fil^{m+q}V_{G}}{\Fil^{n+q}V_{G}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{G}\right)\to H^{2}_{c}\left(\mathcal{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}},\frac{\Fil^{m}V_{H}}{\Fil^{n}V_{H}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{H}\right).

(where we have identified
V_{H}
and
V_{G}
with their own duals, up to twisting).

Remark 0.14.2.1
.

More precisely,
a priori
we have two slightly different versions of the pushforward map. One such map (the one which is “natural” from the point of view of de Rham cohomology) arises from tensoring the short exact sequence of sheaves on
X_{G}

0\to\Omega^{2}_{X_{G}}\to\Omega^{2}_{X_{G}}(\log X_{H})\to\iota_{\star}(\Omega^{1}_{X_{H}})\to 0

with
\Fil^{n+q}V_{G}(-D)
. However, from the point of view of coherent sheaves it is natural to consider instead the sequence of line bundles

0\to\Omega^{3}_{X_{G}}\to\Omega^{3}_{X_{G}}(\log X_{H})\to\iota_{\star}(\Omega^{2}_{X_{H}})\to 0

and tensor with
\Fil^{n+q}V_{G}(-D)\otimes\Omega^{2}_{X_{G}}\otimes(\Omega^{3}_{X_{G}})^{\vee}
. The two constructions are compatible via a map

\iota^{\star}\left(\Omega^{2}_{X_{G}}\otimes(\Omega^{3}_{X_{G}})^{\vee}\right)\otimes\Omega^{2}_{X_{H}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X_{H}}

defined by dualising the natural map

\iota^{*}(\Omega^{1}_{X_{G}})\to\Omega^{1}_{X_{H}}.

We shall be interested in the pushforward map in the form

H^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}},\frac{V_{H}}{\Fil^{r_{1}-q+1}V_{H}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{H}(-D)\right)\to H^{1}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\frac{V_{G}}{\Fil^{r_{1}+1}V_{G}}\otimes\Omega^{2}_{G}(-D)\right).

The sheaf on the right-hand side was denoted by
[\tilde{L}_{1}]
in §6 of
[
LPSZ19
]
, and its cohomology was termed “automorphic nearly-coherent cohomology”. We can expand this to the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}},\frac{V_{H}}{\Fil^{r_{1}-q+1}V_{H}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{H}(-D)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\frac{V_{G}}{\Fil^{r_{1}+1}V_{G}}\otimes\Omega^{2}_{G}(-D)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}},\Gr^{r_{1}-q}V_{H}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{H}(-D)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\Gr^{r_{1}}V_{G}\otimes\Omega^{2}_{G}(-D)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{2}(-D)\right)}

The content of
Proposition

0.14.1.3
is to express the lower diagonal arrow on the direct summands
\omega_{H}^{(t_{1}-t,t_{2})}
and
\omega_{H}^{(t_{1},t_{2}-t)}
of
\Gr^{r_{1}-q}V_{H}
as a multiple of the “standard” pushforward maps from these spaces to
\mathcal{N}^{1}
considered in
[
LPSZ19
, §4.6]
.

We now pass to the
p
-adic completion (i.e. we replace the dagger spaces
\mathcal{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}}
and
\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\mathrm{ord}}
with their underlying rigid-analytic spaces, which amounts to forgetting overconvergence).

Notation 0.14.2.2
.

We denote these spaces by
\mathbb{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1}
and
\mathbb{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}}
.

Then we have the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}\left(\mathbb{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}},\frac{V_{H}}{\Fil^{r_{1}-q+1}V_{H}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{H}(-D)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}\left(\mathbb{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\frac{V_{G}}{\Fil^{r_{1}+1}V_{G}}\otimes\Omega^{2}_{G}(-D)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}\left(\mathbb{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}},\Gr^{r_{1}-q}V_{H}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{H}(-D)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}\left(\mathbb{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\Gr^{r_{1}}V_{G}\otimes\Omega^{2}_{G}(-D)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}\left(\mathbb{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{2}(-D)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}\left(\mathbb{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathfrak{F}(-D)\right).}

Here
\mathfrak{F}=\mathfrak{F}_{G}(3+r_{1},1-r_{2})
is the Banach sheaf introduced in
[
Pil17
]
. The dashed arrow on the right is given by
[
LPSZ19
, Corollary 6.15]
, while the dashed arrow on the left is the unit-root splitting of the Hodge filtration.

Proposition 0.14.2.3
.

The two maps

H^{0}\left(\mathbb{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}},\frac{V_{H}}{\Fil^{r_{1}-q+1}V_{H}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{H}(-D)\right)\longrightarrow H^{1}\left(\mathbb{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathfrak{F}(-D)\right),

given by composing either of the two dashed arrows with the remaining maps in the diagram, coincide.

Proof.

This follows from the argument of Theorem 6.16 of
[
LPSZ19
]
; see Remark 6.18 of
op.cit.
. (In
op.cit.
the cotangent sheaf
\Omega^{1}_{H}\cong\omega^{(2,0)}\oplus\omega^{(0,2)}
was replaced by the conormal sheaf
\ker(\iota^{*}\Omega^{1}_{G}\to\Omega^{1}_{H})\cong\omega^{(1,1)}
, but this makes no difference to the argument.)
∎

We can now summarize the computations of this section in the following corollary:

Corollary 0.14.2.4
.

Let
\eta\in H^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\mathcal{N}^{1})
be a class which is ordinary for the
U_{2,\Kl}^{\prime}
operator, and let
\breve{\eta}
be its unique ordinary lifting to
H^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{G,\Kl}^{\geqslant 1},\Fil^{r_{2}}\mathcal{V}_{G}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{G})
.

Then the linear functional on
H^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}},\frac{V_{H}}{\Fil^{r_{1}-q+1}V_{H}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{H}(-D)\right)
given by pairing with
(\iota_{\Delta}^{[t_{1},t_{2}]})^{*}(\breve{\eta})
factors through the composite of restriction to
\mathbb{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}}
(forgetting overconvergence) and the unit-root splitting into
\Gr^{r_{1}-q}V_{H}
; and it is given on
H^{0}\left(\mathbb{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}},\omega^{(t_{1}-2t,t_{2})}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{H}(-D)\right)
, where
t=r_{2}-q
, by the formula

\frac{(-2)^{q}}{\binom{t_{1}}{t}}\langle\iota_{\star}^{\text{$p$-adic}}(-),\eta\rangle

where
\iota_{\star}^{\text{$p$-adic}}
denotes the pushforward map for
p
-adic modular forms defined in
[
LPSZ19
, §4]
. There is an analogous formula on
\omega^{(t_{1},t_{2}-2t)}
with the factor
\frac{(-2)^{q}}{\binom{t_{2}}{t}}
.

Remark 0.14.2.5
.

We will apply Corollary
0.14.2.4
later to the element
\eta^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\coh,q}
.

Step 4: Computation of the regulator

0.15.
Eisenstein series and Eisenstein classes

0.15.1.
Hecke operators for
\GL_{2}

Let
k\in\mathbf{Z}
. Then we define the space of modular forms for
\GL_{2}
of weight
k
, denoted
M_{k}
, as a
\GL_{2}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{f}})
-module, normalised such that
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&0\\
0&A\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, for
A\in\mathbf{Q}^{+}
acts as
A^{k-2}
. This means that the double-coset operator
\left[\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\varpi_{\ell}&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right]
on the
\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}*&*\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\bmod N\}
invariants coincides with the classical
T_{\ell}
(resp.
U_{\ell}
) if
\ell\nmid N
(resp.
\ell\mid N
).

Remark 0.15.1.1
.

These are the same normalisations as
[
LPSZ19
]
§7.1 and §7.2.

Let
\varpi_{p}
be
p
at the place
p
, and 1 elsewhere. Then we consider the operators on
M_{k}
given by

•

U_{p}=\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\varpi_{p}&i\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
,

•

\langle p\rangle=p^{2-k}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\varpi_{p}&\\
&\varpi_{p}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
,

•

\varphi=p^{1-k}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&\varpi_{p}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.

Note 0.15.1.2
.

(1)

The first two operators preserve the space of forms of level
K_{0}(p^{n})
or
K_{1}(p^{n})
, for any
n\geqslant 1
.

(2)

The operator
\varphi
does not preserve these forms, but sends level
p^{n}
to level
p^{n+1}
.

(3)

The operator
\langle p\rangle
commutes with both
U_{p}
and
\varphi
, and we have
U_{p}\circ\varphi=\langle p\rangle
.

Remark 0.15.1.3
.

Calling this operator “
\varphi
” is a bit abusive since the action of
\GL_{2}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{f}})
is linear (not semilinear). However, this operator agrees with the Frobenius on the forms that are defined over
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
with respect to our
\mathbf{Q}
-model of the Shimura variety.

We shall also need to consider
p
-adic modular forms of weight
k\in\mathbf{Z}
.

Definition 0.15.1.4
.

Letting
Y_{0}(p)
denote the (open) modular curve of level
K^{p}K_{0}(p)
, for some prime-to-
p
level
K^{p}
, we define

\mathcal{M}_{k}(K^{p})=H^{0}\left(\mathcal{Y}_{0}(p)^{\mathrm{ord}},\omega(k;k-2)\right)

where
\mathcal{Y}_{0}(p)^{\mathrm{ord}}
is the ordinary locus as a dagger space.

Note 0.15.1.5
.

For
k\geqslant 0
, the the differential operator

\Theta:\mathcal{M}_{-k}(K^{p})\rTo\mathcal{M}_{k+2}(K^{p})

twists the action of Hecke operators by the
(k+1)
-st power of the norm character. In particular, we have the relations

\mathcal{U}_{p}\circ\Theta=p^{k+1}\Theta\circ\mathcal{U}_{p}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\varphi\circ\Theta=p^{-1-k}\Theta\circ\varphi.

0.15.2.
Eisenstein series

In
[
LPSZ19
, §7.1]
we defined real-analytic Eisenstein series
E^{(r,\Phi)}(-,s)
for
r\geqslant 1
and
\Phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{f}}^{2})
. We define
F^{k+2}_{\Phi}
by setting
r=k+2
and
s=-k/2
. This is a holomorphic modular form if
k\geqslant 1
, or if
k=0
and
\Phi(0,0)=0
; its
q
-expansion is given by

a_{n}\left(F^{k+2}_{\Phi}\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u,v\in\mathbf{Q}\\
uv=n\end{subarray}}u^{k+1}\mathrm{sgn}(u)\Phi^{\prime}(u,v)\quad\text{for $n>0$},

where
\Phi^{\prime}(u,v)=\int_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{f}}}\Phi(u,x)e^{2\pi ixv}\,\mathrm{d}x
.

Remark 0.15.2.1
.

This
F^{k+2}_{\Phi}
is almost the same as the
F^{k+2}_{\phi}
in
[
LSZ17
, Theorem 7.2.2]
; the difference is that we have changed our normalisations for the central characters.

We will be particularly interested in the cases when
\Phi^{\prime}_{p}
is one of the following:

•

spherical
:
\Phi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{sph}}=\mathrm{ch}(\mathbf{Z}_{p}\times\mathbf{Z}_{p})

•

critical
:
\Phi^{\prime}_{\crit}=\mathrm{ch}(\mathbf{Z}_{p}\times\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times})

•

depleted
:
\Phi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{dep}}=\mathrm{ch}(\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}\times\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times})

Note 0.15.2.2
.

If we transport the operators
U_{p},\varphi,\langle p\rangle
over to
\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{2})
compatibly with
\Phi\mapsto F^{k+2}_{\Phi}
, we have
U_{p}\cdot\Phi_{\mathrm{dep}}=0
. Moreover, if
\Phi^{\prime}(x,y)=\mathrm{ch}(A)
for some open compact
A\subseteq\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{2}
, then we have

\displaystyle(\varphi\cdot\Phi)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ch}((1,p)\cdot A),

\displaystyle(\langle p\rangle\cdot\Phi)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=p^{k+1}\mathrm{ch}((p^{-1},p)\cdot A),

\displaystyle(p^{k+1}\langle p\rangle^{-1}\varphi\cdot\Phi)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ch}((p,1)\cdot A).

In particular, this shows that

(1-\varphi)\Phi_{\mathrm{sph}}=\Phi_{\crit},\qquad(1-p^{k+1}\langle p\rangle^{-1}\varphi)\Phi_{\mathrm{crit}}=\Phi_{\mathrm{dep}};

and consequently that
F^{k+2}_{\Phi^{p}\Phi_{\crit}}
is in the
U_{p}=p^{k+1}
eigenspace and
F^{k+2}_{\Phi^{p}\Phi_{\mathrm{dep}}}
in the
U_{p}=0
eigenspace, for any prime-to-
p
Schwartz function
\Phi^{p}
(hence the terminology).

Note 0.15.2.3
.

The Eisenstein series
F^{k+2}_{\Phi^{p}\Phi_{\crit}}
is
p
-adically cuspidal, and hence so is
F^{k+2}_{\Phi^{p}\Phi_{\mathrm{dep}}}
(since the operator
(1-p^{k+1}\langle p\rangle^{-1}\varphi)
will preserve
p
-adic cuspforms).

As in
[
LPSZ19
, §7.3]
, if
\Phi_{p}=\Phi_{\mathrm{dep}}
or
\Phi_{\mathrm{crit}}
, we can construct a
p
-adic modular form

E^{-k}_{\Phi}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{0}(p)^{\mathrm{ord}},\omega^{-k})

of weight
-k
, such that
\theta^{k+1}\left(E^{-k}_{\Phi}\right)=F^{k+2}_{\Phi}
. Clearly the
q
-expansion of this form must be given by

a_{0}+\sum_{n>0}\sum_{uv=n}v^{-1-k}\mathrm{sgn}(u)\Phi^{\prime}(u,v);

and this form is
p
-adically cuspidal if
\Phi_{p}=\Phi_{\mathrm{dep}}
(see Theorem 7.6 of
op.cit.
).

0.15.3.
Eisenstein classes

Notation 0.15.3.1
.

Denote by
Y
the infinite level modular curve.

Write
\mathscr{H}
for the sheaf corresponding to the defining representation of
\GL_{2}
on a modular curve.

Theorem 0.15.3.2

(Beilinson)
.

Let
k\geqslant 1
. There is a
\GL_{2}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{f}})
-equivariant map

\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{f}}^{2},\mathbf{Q})\to H^{1}_{\mot}\left(Y,\Sym^{k}\mathscr{H},1+k\right),\qquad\Phi\mapsto\mathrm{Eis}^{k+2,\Phi}_{\mot},

the
motivic Eisenstein symbol
, with the following property: the pullback of the de Rham realization
r_{\mathrm{dR}}\left(\mathrm{Eis}^{k+2,\Phi}_{\mot}\right)
to the upper half-plane is the
\mathscr{H}^{k}
-valued differential 1-form

-F^{(k+2)}_{\Phi}(\tau)(2\pi idz)^{k}(2\pi id\tau),

where
F^{(k+2)}_{\phi}
is the Eisenstein series defined by

F^{(k+2)}_{\phi}(\tau)=\frac{(k+1)!}{(-2\pi i)^{k+2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in\mathbf{Q}\\
(x,y)\neq(0,0)\end{subarray}}\frac{\hat{\phi}(x,y)}{(x\tau+y)^{k+2}}.

Proof.

See
[
Beĭ86
]
.
∎

Notation 0.15.3.3
.

Let
\Phi^{(p)}\in\mathcal{S}((\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{f}}^{(p)})^{2},\mathbf{Q})
.

•

Write

\mathrm{Eis}_{\NNsyn}^{k+2,\Phi^{(p)},\crit}\in H^{1}_{\NNsyn}(Y_{0}(p)_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}},\Sym^{k}\mathscr{H},1+k)

for the syntomic realisation of the class
\mathrm{Eis}^{k+2,\Phi_{\crit}\Phi^{(p)}}_{\mot}
, and denote by
\mathrm{Eis}_{\lrigsyn}^{k+2,\Phi^{(p)},\crit}
its image under the isomorphism in Theorem
0.9.1.4
.

•

Write
\mathrm{Eis}_{\rigsyn,\mathrm{ord}}^{k+2,\Phi^{(p)},\crit}
for the restriction of
\mathrm{Eis}_{\lrigsyn}^{k+2,\Phi^{(p)},\crit}
to
\mathcal{Y}_{0}(p)^{\mathrm{ord}}
.

•

Write
\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}_{\rigsyn,\mathrm{ord}}^{k+2,\Phi^{(p)},\crit}
for the image of
\mathrm{Eis}_{\rigsyn,\mathrm{ord}}^{k+2,\Phi^{(p)},\crit}
in Gros syntomic cohomology.

All of the above depend
\GL_{2}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{f}}^{(p)})
-equivariantly on
\Phi^{(p)}
.

Remark 0.15.3.4
.

Let
\underline{\Phi}=\left(\Phi_{1}^{(p)}\Phi_{\crit},\,\Phi_{2}^{(p)}\Phi_{\crit}\right)
. Then

\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}_{\rigsyn,\underline{\Phi},\mathrm{ord}}^{[t_{1},t_{2}]}=\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}_{\rigsyn,\mathrm{ord}}^{t_{1}+2,\Phi_{1}^{(p)},\crit}\sqcup\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}_{\rigsyn,\mathrm{ord}}^{t_{2}+2,\Phi_{2}^{(p)},\crit}.

0.15.4.
Reduction to a
p
-adically cuspidal Eisenstein class

Let
V_{H}
be as defined in Section
0.14.1
.
Recall that by Remark
0.9.2.22
we have a pairing, denoted
\langle\quad,\quad\rangle_{\widetilde{\rigfp},\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\Delta}}
,

\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\rigfp,c}\left(\mathcal{X}_{H,\Delta}^{\mathrm{ord}}\langle-\mathcal{D}_{H}\rangle,\mathcal{V}_{H},1+q;P_{q}\right)\times\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rigsyn}\left(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{H,\Delta},\mathcal{V}_{H},2+t_{1}+t_{2}\right)\rTo\mathbf{Q}_{p}.

Aim.
Recall from Corollary
0.12.3.6
that want to compute the quantity

(0.15.1)

\left\langle(\iota^{(t_{1},t_{2})}_{\Delta})^{*}\left(\tilde{\eta}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\rigfp,q,-D}\right),\,\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}_{\rigsyn,\mathrm{ord}}^{t_{1}+2,\Phi_{1}^{(p)},\crit}\sqcup\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}_{\rigsyn,\mathrm{ord}}^{t_{2}+2,\Phi_{2}^{(p)},\crit}\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\rigfp},\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\Delta}},

in terms of coherent cohomology.

The main tool for the evaluation is the Poznań spectral sequence constructed in Propositions
0.12.2.4
and
0.13.1.3
. However, we only have explicit representatives (see (
0.12.11
) and Proposition
0.12.4.5
) of
(\iota^{(t_{1},t_{2})}_{\Delta})^{*}\left(\tilde{\eta}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\rigfp,-D,q}\right)
after replacing
\tilde{\eta}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\rigfp,-D,q}
by its image
\tilde{\eta}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\rigfp,q}\in\widetilde{H}^{3}_{\rigfp,c0}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\Kl},\mathcal{V},1+q;P_{q})
. In order to be able to evaluate (
0.15.1
), we therefore need to replace the Eisenstein class by a version which is
p
-adically cuspidal.

Since rig-fp cohomology is compatible with change of polynomial, we have a natural map

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rigfp}\left(\mathcal{Y}_{0}(p)^{\mathrm{ord}},\Sym^{k}\mathscr{H},1+k;\operatorname{const}1\right)\rTo\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rigsyn}\left(\mathcal{Y}_{0}(p)^{\mathrm{ord}},\Sym^{k}\mathscr{H},1+k\right).

Lemma 0.15.4.1
.

The class
\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}_{\rigsyn,\mathrm{ord}}^{k+2,\Phi^{(p)},\crit}
is in the image of
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rigfp}(\mathcal{Y}_{0}(p)^{\mathrm{ord}};\Sym^{k}\mathscr{H},1+k;\operatorname{const}1)
. In other words, we can lift it to an element
\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}_{\rigfp,\operatorname{const}1,\mathrm{ord}}^{k+2,\Phi^{(p)},\crit}\in\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rigfp}(\mathcal{Y}_{0}(p)^{\mathrm{ord}},\Sym^{k}\mathscr{H},1+k;\operatorname{const}1)
.

Proof.

This is just the statement that the critical-slope Eisenstein series is integrable over the ordinary locus.
∎

Note 0.15.4.2
.

The class
\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}_{\rigfp,\operatorname{const}1,\mathrm{ord}}^{k+2,\Phi^{(p)},\crit}
is
not
in the image of
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rigfp}(\mathcal{X}_{0}(p)^{\mathrm{ord}}\langle-\mathcal{D}\rangle,\Sym^{k}\mathscr{H},1+k;\operatorname{const}1)
– the “degree 1 part” of our fp-pair is cuspidal, but the “degree 0 part” is not – but the constant term of the degree 0 part gets annihilated by
(1-p^{k+1}\langle p\rangle_{\GL_{2}}^{-1}\varphi)
, which corresponds to
1-V_{p}
on
q
-expansions in weight
-k
.

Lemma 0.15.4.3
.

The image of
\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}_{\rigfp,\operatorname{const}1,\mathrm{ord}}^{k+2,\Phi^{(p)},\crit}
under the natural map

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rigfp}\left(\mathcal{Y}_{0}(p)^{\mathrm{ord}},\Sym^{k}\mathscr{H},1+k;\operatorname{const}1\right)\rTo\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rigfp}\left(\mathcal{Y}_{0}(p)^{\mathrm{ord}},\Sym^{k}\mathscr{H},1+k;1-p^{k+1}\langle p\rangle^{-1}_{\GL_{2}}\,t\right)

lifts to a class

\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}_{\rigfp,\cusp,\mathrm{ord}}^{k+2,\Phi^{(p)},\mathrm{dep}}\in\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rigfp}(\mathcal{X}_{0}(p)^{\mathrm{ord}}\langle-\mathcal{D}_{\GL_{2}}\rangle,\Sym^{k}\mathscr{H},1+k;(1-p^{k+1}\langle p\rangle^{-1}_{\GL_{2}}t)).

Proof.

Immediate from Note
0.15.4.2
.
∎

Remark 0.15.4.4
.

These constructions are summarized by the following diagram (where we leave out the coefficients for reasons of space):

{diagram}

Here, the diagonal arrows are given by the formalism for change of polynomial in fp-cohomology. We refer to this as the
herb-chopper diagram
.

0.15.5.
The
\GL_{2}
-Eisenstein class as a coherent fp-pair

We want to find representatives of the image of the class
\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}_{\rigfp,\cusp,\mathrm{ord}}^{k+2,\Phi^{(p)},\crit}
under the map

\displaystyle\alpha_{\GL_{2}}^{-1}:\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rigfp}

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{X}_{0}(p)^{\mathrm{ord}}\langle-\mathcal{D}_{\GL_{2}}\rangle,\Sym^{k}\mathscr{H},1+k;1-p^{k+1}\langle p\rangle^{-1}_{\GL_{2}}\,t\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\rTo^{\cong}H^{1,0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{0}(p)^{\mathrm{ord}}\langle-\mathcal{D}_{\GL_{2}}\rangle,\Sym^{k}\mathscr{H},1+k;1-p^{k+1}\langle p\rangle^{-1}_{\GL_{2}}\,t\right)

constructed in Section
0.13
(c.f. Note

).

.

and

Proposition 0.15.5.2
.

The class
\widetilde{\mathrm{Eis}}_{\rigfp,\cusp,\mathrm{ord}}^{k+2,\Phi^{(p)},\crit}

.

[

describes the coherent fp-pair representing the class

∎

.

We have

U_{p}\left(\epsilon_{0}^{k,\Phi^{(p)}}\right)=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad U_{p}\left(\epsilon_{1}^{k,\Phi^{(p)}}\right)=p^{k-1}\epsilon_{1}^{k,\Phi^{(p)}}.

.
